ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u2	Thank you very much, Mr. Chairman. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! Just a brief explanation, since, at last, the material itself was presented with an explanation to the article and only the material. However, it is definitely a matter of amending the law, which, at a time when the consequences of the economic and financial crisis are still significant, provides for the creation of conditions under which the Ministry of Economy may grant aid to undertakings having a legal status in difficulty, namely the aid of guarantees, which is a good alternative to direct credit at the moment, because it simultaneously promotes the credit activity or activity of commercial banks and, last but not least, the State guarantee, which does not constitute direct and direct financial flows. Therefore, this amendment highlights Article 17, which in fact provides that the conditions under which guarantees are granted or credits and the conditions under which these loans or guarantees are actually granted are changed. Finally, the whole set of measures will in fact be intended for undertakings which have already submitted applications, and they may also be directly assisted by the immediate adoption of the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u9	Thank you very much. The position of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party will be presented by Rado Likar.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u10	Thank you very much. Nice greetings to all present! The aim of the amendment is, according to the applicant, to establish the appropriate legal basis for granting State aid to companies in difficulty, in the form of an instrument of State guarantees. This is supposed to be possible by amending Article 17 of the current law, which provides that the guarantees of the Republic of Slovenia under this law may be granted for the entire amount of credit and that the duration of the individual loan for companies in difficulty should not be less than 5 years and not longer than 10 years. The interest rate shall be determined in such a way that it is comparable to the interest rates owed to the recipients of the highest rating class of the bank. The same applies to credit costs. In order to ensure adequate treatment of applications already received for State aid allocation to firms in difficulty, which is expected to ensure the gradual normalisation of the business to economically bankrupt companies, it is necessary, according to the applicant, to adopt the law in accordance with the urgency procedure. These are six companies, as mentioned above, of which one is one large, three medium-sized and two small companies. Which companies are specifically concerned, the applicant does not specify. This would maintain at least 850 jobs at risk. If, for any reason, the planned State aid allocation process could not be carried out in due course, the existence of these companies and, of course, their employees would be questioned. However, the motioner simply mentions that the amendment of the law also amends article 18 of the law, which lays down cases where the state does not provide a guarantee. The proposal deletes the fourth indent of this Article, which provides that no State guarantee shall be granted to those companies or borrowers that have outstanding liabilities to the State. In its explanation, the applicant excuses this by maintaining this provision for companies to be overly strict and that the removal of this condition can be adequately compensated by a contractual relationship in which a state aid applicant undertakes to pay its obligations to the State first from the funds received. This is, of course, simply a matter of contractual arrangements and no such possibility is provided for in the articles or provisions of the law itself. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will support the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on Aid for the Rescue and Restructuring of Economic Companies in Trouble. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u14	Thank you so much for saying that, Mr. President. Dear representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues! The Law on Aid for rescuing and restructuring companies in difficulty has been the basis for dealing with firms in difficulty since 2002 and has been notified to the European Commission as a rescue and restructuring state aid scheme, which applies until 31. 12. 2013 In the pre-financial and economic crisis period, short-term or long-term credit and non-refundable financial incentives in the form of subsidies were used as key State aid instruments under this Act. According to the applicant, in 2010 and especially in 2011, due to the negative effects of the economic and financial crisis, the demand for such aid increased significantly and, on the other hand, because of budgetary constraints, the State cannot provide funds for credits or subsidies. Although the current law also allows for short- and long-term loan guarantees, this form of aid has not been applied since 2004. The Minister of Economy, in view of the inadequate text of Article 17 of the current law of the State guarantee instrument as State aid measures for firms in difficulty, cannot implement it, as it only provides that the interest rates and the costs of the loans to which the guarantee is granted must be equal to the interest rates and the costs of the same loans to which the Republic of Slovenia owes it, of course this is not possible. The draft law also provides a solution to the possibility that guarantees may be given to undertakings which so far have not been eligible for aid under this law due to outstanding obligations towards the State. In the Slovenian People's Party's Group, we will support the proposal for a law, as we are aware that it is extremely important at this time to maintain any company that has only as good a chance to survive and maintain jobs. However, we hope that, contrary to the practice of one of the Slovenian banks in granting these guarantees, the Ministry will act responsibly and primarily on the same criteria for all aid applicants. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u15	Thank you very much. The positions of the Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners of Slovenia will be presented by Anton Urh.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u16	Thank you for the floor, Mr President, distinguished representatives of the Government, colleagues and colleagues. The Law on aid for the rescue and restructuring of companies in difficulty is being amended by the Government in order to activate additional aid options for bankrupt companies, as the budget lines for the granting of loans to companies in difficulty have been exhausted and the demand for state aid is still increasing. The alternative option as a State aid measure is to provide guarantees that are already legal, but this instrument is not realistic under the existing regime for the current situation. The existing law provides that the interest rate and the cost of the loan must not be higher than those at which that country is indebted. In fact, bank resources are more expensive because the interest rates at which banks themselves are indebted and the funds thus acquired are transferred forward to the economy, higher than those at which the state is indebted. Therefore, it is not realistic to expect the bank to grant a loan whose interest would be lower than those paid on its own. It is therefore an anomaly and illogicality, which is corrected by the amendment under consideration. In this way, the State will be able to grant a guarantee to an undertaking eligible for State aid for a total amount of credit for a period of 5 to 10 years. However, the interest rate and the cost of the loan to which the State will guarantee must be comparable to those to which the highest rating class of banks is subject. Since it will be possible to save several businesses, to preserve jobs and to prevent at least 850 people from unemployment, and thus eventually to save in the state budget, we will confirm the amendment in the DeSUS Group. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u19	Thank you very much. The position of the Parliamentary Club of Liberal Democracy of Slovenia will be presented by Milan Gumzar.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u20	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Colleagues and colleagues! All assembled in the National Assembly are well-welcome. At the Liberal Democracy Club of Slovenia, we will support the proposed amendment to the Law on Aid for the Rescue and Restructuring of Economic Companies in Trouble. Our decision is based on the fact that the law provides for the operationalization of the instrument of state guarantees to companies in difficulty because Slovenia has not yet come out of the crisis and because there is still a significant number of companies in difficulty, we believe that the restructuring process needs to be encouraged and accelerated, including through the smallest measures that help this process. In the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we believe that direct subsidies are not, in principle, or in most cases are not the best instrument to accelerate restructuring, but in most cases it is better to rely on guarantees, subsidies and subsidised loans. This is all the more so for firms in difficulty and where restructuring is needed. The financial injections themselves have proven to be ineffective in the past. However, grants are mainly effective in developing and technological enterprises. And why is this new law needed? In the years 2005-2010, short-term or long-term credit and non-refundable financial incentives in the form of subsidies were used as key State aid instruments under this Act. In 2010, and above all in 2011, there was a significant increase in demand for such State aid due to uncertain economic conditions. As it is said, the law already in force also allows aid in the form of guarantees for short- and long-term loans, but this form of aid has not been applied since 2004. The problem thus arises in implementation, as the law provides that the interest rates and the costs of the guarantees must be equal to the interest rates and the costs of the same types of loans owed to the Republic of Slovenia. However, the interest rates at which banks are in charge of moving their assets forward to the economy are higher than the interest rates at which the State is indebted. The logical conclusion is that it cannot be expected that banks will grant loans whose interest rate is lower than that of the bank. For these reasons, it refers to specific changes which provide that guarantees may be granted for the total amount of credit and that the duration of the individual loan for companies in difficulty shall not be less than five years and not longer than ten years. However, the greatest novelty is in determining the interest rate, which provides that the interest rate and the cost of loans must be comparable to those owed to the recipients of the highest credit rating class of the bank. The new law also has financial implications, which will directly amount to some less than €3 million, while the guarantee potential will be around €10 million. As has already been said, in the PK LDS, we will support the proposal of the law, because we believe that as much of the Slovenian economy as possible must go along the path of further restructuring as soon as possible.

At its 35th meeting, on 31 May 2011, the Economic Committee considered the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Commercial Law as the working body. At its 103rd meeting, on 6 May 2011, the College of Presidents decided that the draft law should be examined in a simplified manner. Representatives of the proposed law, the Ministry of the Economy, the Legal and Legal Service and the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Slovenia participated as usual in the meeting of the Committee. The Committee was informed of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, the Government, the Commission of the State Economic, Commercial, Tourism and Finance Council, the views and proposals of the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Slovenia and the opinion and position of the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Ribnica. All the above-mentioned draft law opposes, and the said Commission of the State Council points out that it is necessary to initiate procedures for such legal arrangements whereby each economic operator should be a member of one of the economic chambers at its own discretion. A representative of the Chamber of Commerce and Industry also took part in the discussion, which stressed that if a decision were to be taken on the abolition of compulsory membership in the Chamber, the law in force should be amended in its entirety, stressing the need for a comprehensive regulation of the functioning of all chambers, but not in a way that everyone would benefit from the conditions created by a particular part of the membership. In the debate, some members of the Committee identified the idea of changing the organisation of the Chambers as topical and considered the text submitted to be too vague and incomplete. It was also pointed out that any change in the organisation of the Chambers and the law of compulsory membership was not only a matter of policy but also of all the Chambers and their membership. In conclusion of the discussion, the Committee took into account the opinion of the Government, the Commission of the State Council, the Legislative and Legal Service, the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Slovenia and the debates on the proposal for a decision that the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Commercial Law was not suitable for further consideration and was also adopted. Thank you.

They also feel that membership and payment of compulsory membership are of no benefit to them at all. Three can be completed in this regard. First time. The applicable Commercial Law prescribes the amount of membership that a member is obliged to pay according to the size of the company. The law allows the Assembly to reduce membership, which is unfortunately not decided in the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Slovenia during the financial and economic crisis. Another time. If the members are dissatisfied with the organisation in which they are involved, whether it is compulsory or voluntary, the first interlocutor to resolve the problem is the organisation’s leadership. And the third time. Much more than voluntary membership is a mandatory membership that justifies the representativeness and objective representation of all stakeholders, in the case of this law, of a small economy. In the DeSUS a Priori Group, we do not reject the possibility of voluntary membership in the Chamber to replace compulsory membership, but we agree that the whole assembly system in the country must be analysed first, and that a systematic and comprehensive solution in this area should be approached on the basis of results. The question also needs to be clarified as to what is the obligation of membership for members themselves, how they are burdened by membership, what good organisation brings them, how the parliamentary mission is carried out with regard to the benefits of those who are expected to receive it, and how the possible shortcomings of the system can be improved for the greater benefit of the members. ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u40	Thank you for your word. Mr President, fellow Members and Members of Parliament! Family relations are currently regulated in the 35-year-old Law on Marriage and Family Conditions. The speciality of family regulation is the fact that in the 1970s this matter was founded on a legal and social modernity, since the law has only been amended three times in force, which has recently been a real rarity. Such a quality and advanced proposal of the Family Code is before you again today, honourable Members and Members. In the proposal for a code we followed the practice of courts, centres for social work, knowledge of theory and international and legal trends. Thus in the code, we have named it a code and not a law, we have fully regulated the whole family law, and in the interest of the child, we strengthen the State's influence on relations within the family. This State aid is mainly shown by the fact that there will be more opportunities for intervention in these relations where this is necessary for the protection and benefit of the child. The benefit of the child was also a basic principle of the code and led to the creation of individual articles. Special emphasis was placed on the establishment of such a legal framework in which the situation of the child will be protected as best as possible in proceedings and even before them. From this point of view, we must first mention the transfer of decision-making measures for the protection and protection of children from social work centres to the courts. The Government has also adopted all the necessary legal bases so that after the adoption of the Code, special family departments can be established in all eleven district courts and procedures will be dealt with as a priority. In this way, we will undoubtedly help to make a more rapid and more unified decision on family matters. We will also achieve the objectives of the Code through the professional and advisory strengthening of social work centres, which will no longer be implementing official acts, but will highlight their basic mission - that is, professional work with the family and the child. In the area of marriages on the initiative of young people, we introduce different ways of concluding a marriage: from a solemn ceremony to a completely informal form of marriage. An important novelty is also the conclusion of property contracts between spouses. On the proposal of non-governmental organisations, the proposal of the Code makes it more appropriate to regulate the situation of adults who, due to development or health problems, are not able to take care of their rights on their own by abolishing the institution of the deprivation of business capacity and the extension of parental rights. Since the rule of peaceful resolution of disputes is undoubtedly one that helps to maintain good relations in the family and to avoid as much suffering as possible, we need to mention a completely new chapter governing prior consultation and family mediation. Quality family relations are, according to the proposaler's assessment, the most appropriate environment for children's development. According to the proposaler's assessment, the family should be viewed from a child's point of view, and from this point of view we also believe that even when a child lives in the same sex community, the recast section on adoptions, in addition to the new possibility of adoption by extra-marital couples, explicitly emphasises the unilateral adoption by same-sex parents. In doing so, the only objective is to regulate the legal situation of children already living in these families. We would also like to re-establish the legal relationship between the same-sex partners by means of a new law, so the proposal for a law provides for two new institutions: a partnership and a non-partnership community, where, in accordance with the Constitutional Court's decision, the legal status of same-sex partnerships, except in the common adoption segment – so we do not create new same-sex families – in this way we compare with the legal consequences of a marriage or an extra-legal community. Dear Members and Members. On this occasion, I invite you to support also the government amendment, which re-establishes the definition of the non-legislative community, as proposed in the basic version of the Code. The National Assembly has adopted the proposal of the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group of Ambassadors and such a change is considered unacceptable by the Government. The registration of the non-legislative community proposed by the supplement is not in accordance with the informal nature of the non-legislative community. This change in definition also constitutes an unacceptable intervention in the rights of non-marital partners who do not wish to decide to register their relationship, while also affecting a number of [[...]] [[sign for the end of the debate]] Finally, I might also mention that an institution is being set up to support the child. In fact, the entire Family Code is intertwined with the need for child protection and protection, so I invite you to support it at the end.

She says: "the family is a living community of children with either one or both parents or with another adult, if it takes care of the child..." and so on. Well, the family is a little different than it has been defined so far. An old 1976 law, which was never disputed in this regard, says: "The family is a living community of parents and children who enjoy special protection for the benefit of children." Article 53 of the Constitution states that the State shall protect the family, motherhood and paternity. ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u44	Thank you very much for your word, Madam President. Representatives of the Ministry, colleagues and colleagues! The theme is undoubtedly important. We all come from such or different living and family communities and live in them for the most part. Our families are different. Classic, single parent, reorganized, extended, foster, adoptive, also same-sex. Our living communities are sometimes formalized and secondly not formal. They're different, just like the rest of us. And in order to capture all the diversity and complexity of the family life of our social reality, the proposal of the Family Code summarises the universal definition of family, which was adopted by the United Nations in 1994. A recognized group of experts led by Professor Wilfried Dumon of the Catholic University of Belgium, Leuvn, wrote this definition, which does not discriminate against anyone, takes nothing, endangers no one and devalues nothing. This is the universal definition of a family by which the family is a community of adults or persons caring for children or children. There's no family without children. The basic content of each family is its content. According to the Family Code, this is a parental concern, a relationship of trust, concern, security, and love, and not the form or form of this family. Only by such an inclusive definition can we equally protect the rights of all children, regardless of the form of the family they live in. Such a solution is much closer to reality, given that today's family communities, which exist, do not have an equivalent legal and formal basis, in view of the application of all the rights and obligations in force in the classical family, are characterised by great diversity in the composition of the so-called family members. This Act establishes the separation of the professional advisory work of social work centres from the decision-making phase on the necessary measure, including the decision on the care, care and adoption of a child belonging to the judicial domain. We welcome the introduction of the Institute of Child Defenders and the explicit prohibition of corporal punishment of children and other degrading treatment, thereby fulfilling the commitments of the Convention on the Rights of the Child. The law in the work relating to the regulation of relationships between partners introduces many improvements that will help to facilitate and speed up the resolution of mutual relations when the relationship ends. The contribution to this will certainly be a marriage contract; fewer disputes before the courts and quicker resolution of conflicts between couples that will break up will also be due to the possibility of prior consultation and family mediation, and the law will also set out the principle of protecting the weaker from partners. In the second reading of the Code we received 4 clear and separate definitions of partner relationships and consequently derived almost formal legal alignment of the effects of these links with the exception of adoption of so-called "foreign" children in same-sex relationships. This compromise solution for the regulation of the definitions of partnerships and their origins of mutual rights and obligations means the convergence of reality, constitutionally ordered rights and also of public opinion. Given that Slovenian society, as it has been shown, has not yet matured to adopt a fully liberal approach without reservations to the equal settlement of issues in the various types of partnership communities - that is to say, full equality in the section on adoption - Members of the DeSUS would, in principle, consider it good that, for the sake of the fate of the whole code, which is good and that its adoption is for the benefit of children and also for the protection of the weaker partner in relation to the urgent, we support the Code in the form it is written, but we have doubts about the majority opinion of Slovenian citizens and citizens. Voting on the Family Code in our parliamentary group will not be uniform, because we will vote in our conscience and conviction.

Modern understanding of family, children's needs is broader than some would like to show, is an open concept of real life, the protection of children's rights in all relationships, which should be allowed and modern understanding is also a legal correct summary, regulation of real social relations. ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u56	Mr Vice-President, Madam Secretary of State with a co-worker, colleague and colleague! The family code that we meet at the final stage of its adoption has a long history. He began to form at the beginning of the former government’s term of office, and see it — in the current version, the definition of a family, which is totally identical to what we are meeting today, was not at that time controversial. Preparations continued at the beginning of this mandate in cooperation with key family and criminal law experts. The process of public and political treatment of the Family Code has been under way for over a year and a half. According to public responses and performances, the content of the main part of the code, as provided for in 303 articles, is satisfactory for most of the participants in this process. Comments and strong opposition to this code focus mainly on two, we can say, ideological content: the changed definition of family and the rights of same-sex partners. The differences aren't professional, they're two-way collisions. A view that acknowledges that there is a difference in society and accepts it. The view that it recognises that life patterns in society are changing makes sense to include them in legislation in order to ensure the same rights for everyone in society. This is a view that we also want to follow in the Group of Social Democrats. On the other hand, we are faced with a view that rejects everything that does not belong in the traditional social mold of family practices and understands the acceptance of diversity as a threat and an attack on our way of life and as an evaluation of the traditional family. The dynamics of the development of family practices in Slovenia are nothing different than elsewhere in the world. In recent decades, families seem to be moving away from the classic model of a nuclear family, which began to lose weight in the 1970 ’ s, and even more so in the 1980 ’ s. As a result, it places the process of pluralisation of family forms at the centre of family debates, covering more and more parallel processes. Reducing the number of nuclear or traditional families, increasing the number of reorganized, single parents and, yes, single-sex families. Several statistics have already been mentioned to say that in fact, the main pattern of family life is beginning to be formed mainly in the extra-marital community, in which 53% of the children were born to break up each fourth marriage, which means that either single-parent families, which are already more than one-fifth as well as reorganised families, are being formed. That there are also married couples in Slovenia, which is 20% who do not have children. So it is also unnecessary to want to order that the fundamental task of the marriage is reproduction and the creation of a new life. These are all the facts and professional arguments that they have identified and introduced into their legislation in countries that we know as the most democratic, such as those that respect human rights and children's rights: Sweden, Norway, the Netherlands, Belgium, the United Kingdom, Iceland, if we count everything, we see that they already represent a quarter of the countries of the European Union, each year a new country joins them. Don't they recognise family rights in these countries? Don't they respect children's rights? Yes, indeed, but they also recognize the diversity and recognize that everyone who is different from us has the same rights as us. Well, even in our debate, these facts and professional arguments have been a key guide that we advocates of the Family Code have reiterated in a broad public and political debate that has lasted more than 68 hours so far. Regrettably, in the debate, professional arguments have repeatedly reaffirmed the ideological justifications of opponents of the proposal, including in the form of an aggressive campaign aimed at granting the marriage of women and men in our society a special, privileged status in relation to other forms of family life that have developed over the past 30 years, and that the same-sex community is fully excluded from the proposal of the Family Code. Only in this way could the Family Code, in their view, become socially acceptable. Such a debate by Slovenia, which on the one hand seeks to be an open and tolerant society, leads back. Moreover, the debate is on the verge of undermining the liberal values of family policy, which have become an integral part of our country's rule of law in the 1970s, to which we can rightly be proud. As early as 1976, Slovenia aligned the rights of non-legislative communities with the marriage union and allowed, in the spirit of an open and advanced society, the coexistence and development of various forms of family communities. And what happens to us 35 years later? First, in the proposal for the Family Code, provisions have become unacceptable to regulate the rights of same-sex unions and those of living children. In order to oppose the public and to ensure that the Family Code, which fundamentally regulates the protection of children's rights and represents a major step forward in the protection of these rights, is adopted as soon as possible, we have drawn up a so-called compromise proposal and solutions which we have assessed to be more acceptable to the general public. In short, we were able to take the courage to step back so that we could reach the desired goal as soon as possible. Which, of course, we cannot claim for those who continue to oppose the Family Code. Thus, we have defined the marriage union, or extramarital community, in the latest version of the Family Code proposal, only as a community of men and women. However, similar communities of same-sex partners, with virtually equal rights, have been named by the terms of the partnership and the non-partnership community and defined in specific articles. The same-sex couples will be able to adopt their partner's child and the new version of the law no longer foresees the so-called mutual adoption of same-sex partners, i.e. adoption of children whose biological parent is not one of the same-sex partners. In doing so, I would like to point out that the Social Democrats have agreed to a compromise because we have assessed that the situation in Slovenian society is not yet such that the adoption of children by the same-sex partners is fully balanced with the diversified ones. Nevertheless, the compromise allows us to realize the basic purpose of the Family Code, that is to protect the rights of all children, regardless of the type of family they live in. This will eliminate discrimination against children who already live in same-sex families and do not have many rights belonging to children living in traditional families. The right to inheritance and family pension after the deceased partner, the right to survival, partner insurance and the right to make contact. The current regime is not in accordance with the Convention on the Rights of the Child, which provides that the welfare and benefit of the child in regulating the highest and absolute criterion. Also for children living in same-sex partnerships. Let me also say that when we swear on children's rights: do we not allow these measures, when we do not want to equalize these children with other children, to create 21st century pancretes? Regrettably, the amendments introduced by the compromise proposal restricting the rights of same-sex partners remain too small for opponents of the proposal of the Family Code to support the compromise proposal. More! In addition to the same-sex partner communities, traditional family advocates have chosen this type of community for a new problem, as we have said, as 53% of the relationships with children have chosen this type of community. We believe that the establishment of the institution of a registered non-legislative community is controversial from a professional point of view, because it cannot be implemented in practice due to inadequacy, it is also unacceptable from a cultural and civilisation perspective, as such proposals of the opposition interfere with the rights of non-legislative communities acquired 35 years ago. And people have made it very clear that they consider this an intervention in their acquired rights and do not accept it. Let me also remind you that the idea of second-reading applicants was to achieve discrimination against these communities by means of the amendment in such a way that non-legislative partners would not have the right to adopt children. In the third reading, the Social Democrats will support the amendment of the Government to Article 4, by which we will introduce the original definition of the non-legislative community into the Family Code. The proposal by the opposition amendment, which, due to the majority opposition to the public, wants to amend the provision on the registration of the non-legal union, remains professional and ideologically controversial and is rejected by the Social Democrats. The registration of the non-legislative community or a different form of formalisation of this institution is unnecessary, even though the proposal for a new amendment by the SDS Group and other partners is defined as optional. We ask Social Democrats what the added value of this new proposal is, and we are not alone in this. In short, it is also a matter of profession and, last but not least, our Legal and Legal Service. Let me conclude that there are several reasons for the adoption of the Family Code, but the common objective of improving the protection of children's rights is in particular to all. The view adopted by the Code, which we also wish to follow in the Group of Social Democrats, recognises that there is a difference in society that we accept and recognise that life patterns in society are not given once and for all. The solutions in the proposal of the Family Code do not threaten anyone, at least the Slovenian family and Slovenian people, as we had the opportunity to hear. The law recognises and allows for a difference, but it does not promote or promote it. It brings supplementation, enrichment and regulation to what has not yet been arranged. If a person takes a person’s right, it is certainly not the one we accept differently. We estimate that through the compromise solutions that we have entered into the Family Code, we have drawn sufficiently close to the thinking of most of the Slovenian public and, on the other hand, have taken an important step forward in efforts to eliminate discrimination against same-sex partners and their children in particular. That is why the Social Democrats will support the proposal of the Third reading Family Code. Thank you very much.

These fundamental principles and needs are confirmed by the General Declaration on Human Rights in Article 16, the International Covenant on Economic, Social and Cultural Rights in Article 10 and the International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights, Article 23. However, the most recent international instrument in this area is the Convention on the Rights of the Child, which provides, inter alia, that States parties must recognise to each child the right to a standard of living which corresponds to their physical, mental, spiritual, health and social development, with one or both parents having the primary responsibility to ensure, in accordance with their capacities and material capacities, the living conditions necessary for the child to develop. However, the signatory countries must take all appropriate measures to help the parents or others responsible for the child to exercise this right and, if necessary, to provide material assistance and other assistance programmes. Even in our constitution, we can already hear, it is beautifully written: "The state protects the family, motherhood, paternity, child and youth and creates the necessary conditions for this protection.", this is recorded in Article 53, paragraph 3. And in Article 54, the rights and duties of parents, the first paragraph states: "Parents have the right and the duty to maintain, educate and raise their children". We would expect the government to propose measures that will facilitate the coordination of family care and service obligations for parents, allow for a more appropriate moral and material evaluation of work with children and a more equitable distribution of responsibility for them, for example through measures such as professional services to help the family at home, to protect children at home, to help the elderly and the sick at home, to help the disabled at home, to help the disabled at home, to help the household at home, which would be urgently needed in our society and would greatly facilitate the care of the family for all. We have also called in writing to this effect for the President of the Government of Mr Pahor, and the coalition insists stubbornly. The existing law governing marriage and family clearly states that marriage is an important part of the family arrangement. On the contrary, the proposal for a code does not provide for any meaning, purpose or purpose of marriage, except for the interrelationship of the partners. ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u87	Thank you very much. Radovan Žerjav. He's already taken his time. Sylven Tiny. Mr Radovan Žerjav has the floor. Let's get Sylven Tiny ready.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u88	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. I haven't taken advantage of my time, I haven't spoken to you yet today, but we still have some time in the Slovenian People's Party. It is true, we have talked a lot about this Family Code recently, but little has been devoted to the legal aspects of the regulation of the main institutions of family relations, such as the marriage which the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has fundamentally amended in Article 3 of the Code. As is known, with the possibility of marriage also for the same sex, the legal possibility of adoption of children by same-sex communities will be legal. The reasons for such a radical change in the marriage union in the proposal of the Code are not legally valid and the consequences for protection, and the benefits of the child are not adequately explored. We do not have sufficient scientific research on what this means for a child. Therefore, it makes sense to maintain the existing arrangement of marriage as a community of husband and wife, the importance of which is to establish an environment of interrelationship and affection between the partner and at the same time in the design of the offspring. The current regime is consistent with the Constitution and any referendum decision on this issue should not be disputed in any way. Indeed, the existing arrangement of marriage and its legal relations is indeed well founded. Of course, the fundamental interest of each country, the state community, is natural reproduction. This is by nature possible only between partners of different sexes. The maintenance of the institution of marriage as a community of husband and wife is in line with the strictest standard of human rights protection at home and in the world. The right to marriage in the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia is linguistically and systematically regulated, comparable to the Council of Europe Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. Both in the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia and in the Council of Europe Convention, marriage is not directly defined and is subject to an identical provision on equality before the law, which prevents discrimination on the basis of personal circumstances, including sexual orientation. In this context, the European Court of Human Rights in Strasbourg has repeatedly decided that the purpose of the right to marriage is to enable men and women to form a legally binding living community of two adults. The European Court has made it clear that the Council of Europe Convention protects the traditional form of marriage, the husband and the wife. Following a court decision, this right does not extend to other forms of adult living communities, such as the same-sex partner community. This position has been adopted by the European Court of Human Rights in many cases. The doctrine of the right to marriage and family as an exclusive right of men and women has also not been abandoned by the court in the latest case law, as was misrepresented by the Slovenian public in the public debate on the proposal of the Family Code in the National Assembly. The concept of marriage as a husband and wife union was indirectly followed by the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia when deciding on the constitutionality of the Law on the Registration of the same-sex partnership. In doing so, the Constitutional Court did not find unconstitutionality in the regulation of marriage as a community - exclusively a man and a woman. When assessing constitutionality, it was limited to the constitutionality of the regulation of the right to inherit persons in registered same-sex partnerships. And despite the jurisdiction under the law, the Constitutional Court did not intervene in the area of the constitutionally guaranteed right to marriage and family. The Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia follows the case-law of the European Court of Human Rights in its understanding of the marriage and of the husband and wife, which is expected in view of the comparable regulation of the right to marriage in the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia and in the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. The appropriateness of the definition of marriage as a husband and wife's community is also confirmed by the linguistic and deliberate interpretation of the United Nations General Declaration on Human Rights in articles 16 and 13 of the United Nations International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights. These international instruments explicitly stipulate that the right to enter into a marriage belongs to men and women. It is not possible to imagine that, at the time of the creation of these international instruments, the signatory State would understand, under the terms of marriage, something other than the community of men and women, and even today, such an understanding of this legal institution still enjoys the broadest support in the international community. The marriage, as a community of husband and wife, is further legalized in us geographically and culturally closest countries, such as Austria, Germany, Italy and other Central European countries. This does not change the decision on the rejection of the German Constitutional Court of August this year, which, by way of explanation, refers to the possibility of adopting a biological child as an same-sex partner, but does not refer in any way to adoptions on average. This decision was also misinterpreted by supporters of the Family Code proposal. So today it is mainly ideology and literally rape some of the solutions that the nation, or, if I am more precise, part of the nation, the majority of the nation, does not want. He won't and won't accept. It is again a division that we are introducing into Slovenian society, according to my assessment at this time completely unnecessary. And I say that these solutions, some that are unacceptable to us, all the others — the great majority — are of course good, those that are not; perhaps we have not all matured yet so that we can accept them and it would be my opinion that at this point, although I am aware that this is no longer possible, but that we would have removed them. Family today in these critical times means everything and more. With the loss of jobs and other problems that citizens and citizens have in this crisis, in these times, only these are found mainly in the family, there seeking support and relief for their pains, psychological and other. Today, however, the family is supposed to be simply anything and this is not acceptable to us and cannot be acceptable. Some in this room have insulted the institution of the family, have insulted, and I will not talk about specific cases, but the family can't really be anything and it would be nice if those who think that anything could also respect the opposite opinion of those who think that it simply can't be anything. Where does the country today positively discriminate against the family, where? Give me one concrete example where this country, this community, positively discriminates against the family. Do you know that it happens that unfortunately successful marriages or people who have long lived together in marriage are separated because it is simply worth it. It's paid off financially and it's not normal, it's not a normal company. In order to separate the two who continue to live together from each other in order to perhaps obtain more resources from the State through social transfers, such and other reliefs, for their "family", because this allows the country. Is that normal? Would it not be more normal for the family in this case to be positively discriminated against and that those living in families, but by the State, would be treated differently than those living in 'corn'? That would have been a logical move, and it would have been normal, but obviously nothing in this state is normal. So in any case, I won't support this marriage. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u118	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. At its 30th meeting, on 25 May this year, the Committee on Culture, Education, Sport and Youth considered a proposal to adopt an authentic interpretation of Article 36 of the Law on the Realisation of Public Interest in Culture, submitted by Mr Zmago Jelinčič Plemeniti. The Committee took note of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service and of the opinion of the Government. Mr Jelinčič Trimeniti, the proponent, introduced a supplementary explanation to the proposal for an authentic explanation. He said that, on 28 April 2011, a proposal for this authentic interpretation had been tabled in the parliamentary procedure in order to prevent the discharge of SNG Director Drama Iva Ban, which was carried out on 24 May 2011. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service said that two questions had to be answered when adopting an authentic interpretation, namely the question of the need for admission and the question of admissibility, in relation to the proposed content, were to be answered. The requirement for the adoption of an authentic interpretation is ambiguity of the legal norm and the applicant's argument raises the question of the appropriateness of the institution of authentic interpretation, since in its application, as in the case of the adoption of laws, the legislator must be based on the principles of generality and abstraction. The assessment of the circumstances of a particular procedure shall be subject to legal proceedings within the framework of the guaranteed right to judicial protection. The authentic interpretation is also not substantively appropriate. The question of the appropriateness of transferring powers to a minister with a separate hierarchical lower act than the law in the case of the proposed authentic interpretation is not relevant, since the provisions of the Act of Establishment merely constitute the implementation of the rules of procedure in accordance with the competences arising otherwise from the provisions of the Law on the Realisation of the Public Interest in Culture. It also considered that the proposed text of the authentic interpretation was not appropriate, it was not clear enough, since when it claims that the legal regime was unclear or inadequate, it was referring to the regulation of jurisdiction in a law that did not specify it at all. At the same time, it does not solve the problem identified by the applicant as a reason for accepting an authentic explanation. After the introductory part, the Committee referred to the question of whether the proposed authentic interpretation of the law should be adopted. In the context of the debate, the question of the suitability and admissibility of the SNG Director Drama Iva Ban was most often raised. Some considered that this was inappropriate, while others considered that the decision establishing the institution, which was adopted under Article 39 of the Law on the Realisation of Public Interest in Culture, governed the procedure for the appointment and discharge of the director and did not confer on the Minister of Culture the power to appoint and dispose of the director, but was given that power by the Law on the Realisation of the Public Interest in Culture. The follow-up was further explained by a government representative, and after the discussion had been concluded, the Committee adopted a decision of eight votes and seven against: the Committee on Culture, Education, Sport and Youth considers that there is no need to adopt an authentic interpretation of Article 36 of the Law on the Realisation of Public Interest in Culture. In view of this, the Committee on Culture, Education, Sport and Youth proposes that, pursuant to Article 151 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, it should not adopt a proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 36 of the Law on the Realisation of Public Interest in Culture. This has also been put into decision by the National Assembly. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u119	Thank you. I give the floor to the Government's representative for the introductory explanation of the government. Mr. Stoyan Pelko, Secretary of State, please, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u120	Dear Members, Madam Ambassador! Let me briefly present the government's opinion. In my opening remarks, I would like to say that you are not even discussing or voting today on the responsibility of the director of a public institution, as the Council of the Public Institute has argued in a reasoned manner, and the general public is also rightly discussing. Today you are discussing and voting on the persuasiveness of the proposal to adopt an authentic explanation. Here, both the professional services of our ministry and the Government of Law Service, as the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly, are completely unified, so that this proposal is unnecessary and unacceptable, because there is absolutely no uncertainty in the legal dictatorship. Therefore, allow me only this explanation, by which the Government has already argued on the Committee on Culture, why it is not necessary to adopt an authentic explanation. ZUIK, namely, the Law on the Realisation of Public Interest in Culture in Article 536, provides that, if the founder of a public institution is a State, he shall tender and appoint the Director of the Minister responsible for Culture. Furthermore, Article 37 provides for reasons why the Director of the Public Institution may be dismissed. Article 39 of the ZUIK provides that an act establishing a public institution must specify the procedure in more detail. Therefore, if a person considers that the void in paragraph 5 of Article 36, which provides that the Director is appointed by a Minister, but does not explicitly provide for the discharge of the Director, it is sufficient to look only at Article 38 of the Law on Institutions, which provides for the discharge of the Director by the competent authority. And since the competent authority for the appointment of a Director under Article 36 ZUIK is a Minister or Minister, it is also responsible for his discharge. We mentioned on the committee that there is also case law, for example, in the Supreme Court of the Republic of Slovenia, number 1383/2002. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u121	Thank you. The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. Mr Franco Juri will present the position of the Zares Group.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u122	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Mr. Secretary of State, hello! Colleagues and colleagues! The request by the Member of the European Parliament for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of Article 36 of the Law on the Realisation of Public Interest in Culture relates to current developments. And that is why such a proposal is controversial and questionable. The Member of the Minister's delegation is concerned about the discharge procedure of Director Drama, Mr Ivo Ban, which is carried out under the act of establishment. It argues that the authority of the Director's discharge authority must be determined in accordance with the law and that the law governs only the execution of the call and the appointment of the Director, not the discharge. The discharge authority was granted to the Minister by means of an act establishing it, which, in his view, is contrary to the law. The opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service is crystal clear on this point: it is not possible to understand from the explanation of the proposal for the adoption of an authentic interpretation the clear and explicit assessment of the applicant that the provision of Article 36, paragraph 5, of the ZUIK is in fact unclear. The comparative review shows that the competence to appoint according to its content covers both the right to decide on the appointment and the right to decide on the discharge. The competence for appointment and discharge, combined in one body, is a well-established practice in legislative practice. In their view, the authentic interpretation is also not materially appropriate. Therefore, the Group of Members does not consider that the proposed authentic interpretation is also appropriate on the basis of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service and we will support the Committee's decision.

Only if the dispute or ambiguity of an individual provision is found to be relevant will an authentic interpretation be taken into account, and in this case the proposal is clear that it is not necessary. According to our assessment, the legislator's will in article 36 is quite clear: despite the fact that the statutory provisions do not explicitly write this down, the Minister also has the power to resolve what he himself appoints, since it is so logical that the power to appoint by its content covers such a right to decide on the appointment as well as the right to decide on the discharge. ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u134	Mr President, thank you so much for your words. Dear Members and Members! The substance of the proposed amendment, the additions to the Electoral Law to the National Assembly, is the application of Article 80 of the Constitution of Slovenia, supplemented in 2000, by the provision that, under the proportional electoral system, a 4 % electoral threshold should ensure the decisive impact of voters on the allocation of seats to candidates. The unanimous expert opinion has resulted in the solution before you today. This is achieved by maintaining the constituencies only at administrative level as an element for the conduct of elections. Eight constituencies, which are fully maintained, will elect 11 Members or Members of Parliament in each of them. A single list of candidates or list proposed by a political party will have to be made available to 11 candidates within the electoral unit, of which five will be candidates. The electoral guide will be as follows; it will be mandatory to vote by surrounding the name and name before the name of the individual candidate on the list, the candidate who will reach the highest number of votes from this list will come to Parliament. An absolute mandatory preferred vote is therefore introduced. The so-called decisive vote. In Parliament, therefore, the Members and Members of Parliament, who will reach the majority of voters in this constituency within one of the electoral units on each of the lists. In order to avoid an excessive number of invalid ballots in the event that the voter gives the vote to the list, the order, such as the candidates and the candidates listed on the list, will not be taken into account, since this vote will not go to anything, the law assumes that the vote is also valid in this case, but that vote will be divided into one eleven and will not have a decisive impact on the election. So the assumption doesn't change the order. A decisive impact will be achieved in the way that voters will absolutely decide which of the eleven candidates of each party within the constituency will be elected to Parliament. Another important solution that we have drawn up in cooperation with representatives of the Italian and Hungarian national communities is the change of the so-called Electoral System Board to a one-way majority electoral system, which is simpler, in this part also raises the number of signatures with which voters, members of the Italian and Hungarian national communities determine the candidate as a Member. The third solution by which we eliminate the anti-constitutional situation, as established by the Constitutional Court, is to regulate voting by post, to supplement the legal void in the same way as only the Constitutional Court has proposed in a constitutional decision. We are introducing an extremely friendly way for voters, an example that we see in neighbouring countries: in the Republic of Austria, Germany, as well as in England. All who have a permanent or temporary residence in the Republic of Slovenia will be able to express within fifteen days before the elections an administrative unit, where they have a permanent or temporary residence, the desire to vote by post, in which case the material will be sent home, counted as the other ballots. Even if such an electorate were to change his mind and go to the polls today, he would be able to vote if he handed out an electoral card to him by mail. Equal participation in elections also increases the proportion of the representation of one sex, in particular women's sex in elections. The percentage now laid down in the law 35 is up to 40, which means one candidate more on the list. It will have to be placed there by a party. In a symbolic sense, they will be classified in the form of an alternate classification, and in four of the eight constituencies, one gender will have to be represented first. There are still many changes that we have made as improvements in cooperation with the professional public and the State Election Commission. I suggest you support the proposed solutions. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-06-16-SDZ5-Redna-29.ana.u136	Thank you for the floor, Mr President. Dear Minister with co-worker, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! Today we are considering a bill that provides for amendments to the electoral legislation for the election of Members to the National Assembly. The most important reasons for the amendments mentioned by the applicants stem from the desire for greater personalisation of elections, which one of the decisions of the Constitutional Court is supposed to require. As can be seen from the assessment of the situation and the reasons for the adoption of the law referred to by the proposer, the whole matter is practically based on the words, I quote: "The Members, other than Members of the national community, shall be elected in accordance with the principle of proportional representation at 4 % of the electoral threshold for entry into the National Assembly, with voters having a decisive impact on the allocation of seats to candidates." Your Honor, this is a dictatorship that is supposed to convince us Members that the current electoral system is weak. As a result of the Constitutional Court's decision, we are obliged to amend the legislation, at least so in the Slovenian People's Party we understand the Government's argument. But what are voters currently doing in today's system, or are they not choosing among the candidates today? So, are we Members elected just like that, by chance? Are we not also Members of the party's face on the local scene? What, then, is the voter's decisive impact on the mandate? The voter has the opportunity to decide between the candidates of all political parties in a given district and has the option, in his opinion, to choose the most suitable candidate. Isn't that a decisive influence? The main difference between the current electoral system and what you are proposing is that since the borders will no longer play exactly any role, there will certainly be an increase in the speculative determination of candidates at the level of the electoral unit by political parties. I do not know what the practice of other political parties is, but I can say for the Slovenian People's Party that we are always trying to find a candidate who comes from the constituency in which he is running, and is recognized there by his work and participation in social life and environment. With the current arrangement, it has the opportunity to demonstrate its abilities before its local environment and then to be selected by the local environment as its representative in the National Assembly. With a new proposal, we create, in a way, a political alliance and marketing that is not based on good work on the ground among people. In time, political parties will, of course, start by using the grave economic term, maximising their earnings in the form of votes in the constituency area and no longer in the district. This is a perfectly logical and legitimate consequence, but, as we stress in the Slovenian People's Party, it will no longer be important from which region or region is a candidate for a Member. Candidates who carry votes at the level of the electoral unit and no longer the district will be put at the forefront. Only Ljubljana with its surroundings is entangled in as many as two constituencies, and other constituencies have urban centres. If only an absolute number of votes are counted, candidates from larger cities will, of course, be preferred. They are simply followed by more people, as they are also active in the field of multiple populations. With the current system, however, such an effect, according to the beliefs of the Slovenian People's Party, is somewhat eliminated and, more importantly, we also attach the candidate to the voter's simultaneous trust in the party. The proposed regulation will therefore encourage political parties to redistribute the candidates within the electoral unit fully, as the borders will no longer play any role or will only be administrative. This, of course, completely destroys the high reputation or, if you want, the rating of an individual in his local environment, and replaces him with someone who is more recognizable in the national scale. What is worst in this arrangement is that it is not necessary that the visibility of the latter is derived from its work and participation in the political space. We often hear among citizens that in politics we need people with real-sector experience, we hear that in politics we need people with real-sector experience who have worked in an economy who know what things are in practice, the situation on the market and so on. With these changes proposed by the Government, we will certainly not be able to do so. If I do a little cartooning, we can rightly expect, after the proposed legislative changes, that the policy will also try to engage some successful athlete, media personality, player, TV leaders, journalists and so on. This has happened before, but not always successfully. While such a candidate may not be able to obtain a high percentage of the votes in one of the polls, all the votes resulting from its recognition in the 11 circuits are already counted as something. Nor does the legislation prevent such a candidate from giving up his post later in the event of an election to a Member State; this has already happened that they have renounced it. However, the votes he received will remain with the parties and the speculative intent will be repaid. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party, we so rightly estimate that the proposed regime allows for much more speculation and manipulation of the electorate's will than has been possible so far. Of course, the current electoral system is not ideal either, but in the world it would be difficult to find one that could be called perfect. For the Slovenian People's Party, in the current regime of the electoral system, it is particularly important to allow for a significantly greater and more equitable regional representation in the National Assembly, which is not possible for all the proposals you have been able to draw up so far, including what we now have on the table. In the proposed electoral regime, proposed by the amendment to the law by abolishing electoral thefts, it would certainly be advantageous in the long term for candidates from larger urban centres and for the media more recognizable faces, and for candidates from the outskirts and less populated areas there would be virtually no more opportunities for election to the National Assembly. Such a solution certainly does not contribute to the decentralisation and even regional development of Slovenia, and for which we have been taking the lead in the Slovenian People's Party all the time. And the truth is, in recent times, Slovenia has also developed solidly evenly, even though we wanted significantly more. In the Republic of Slovenia we also have a so-called proportional system of elections. The system, sometimes so criticized, is now again in force in the oldest world democracies, as it turns out that the long-standing exchange of only two political options in power at a certain time also wears off or disembowels. Therefore, in the Slovenian People's Party we support such a system because it allows new, fresh ideas to come and also facilitate the arrival of new people, new faces into political space. And let's not forget, we don't have a tradition yet, 20 years is just behind us. And I think we still have time for everything to come to its right place in the long run. However, as such, the system cannot guarantee complete personalisation, because if this is to be achieved, then the electoral system should be completely changed or replaced. With only partial corrections, as anticipated by the marriage novelties, we will do more harm than good. We see no problem in this, especially if minority representatives agree. The problem already arises with the proposed amendment or simplification of the post-vote system, where it is for every citizen to decide on such a way of voting without any justification. The applicant's statements on experiences from abroad show that the abuse of voting votes by mail is not detectable. This is an argument without an empirical and professional basis that doesn't convince us at all.
